Hi. Yeah, um, well, I guess what made me come in is what happens when I get home after work or class. I've been taking some college courses while working as a financial manager, which is a lot of pressure for a 19-year-old, I know. My boss is always throwing these massive spreadsheets at me right at 4:45 PM, which is just so inconsiderate, right? Like, people want to go home! 

But anyway, I'll finally get home, and I'll sit down with my laptop—usually to watch a show or something, I'm really into that new interior design competition on Netflix right now, the hosts are hilarious—and I'll just end up eating a whole takeout meal. Plus chips, and sweets, and literally whatever snacks are hiding in my desk drawer. It's way, way more than I ever planned to eat. And definitely way more than I’d ever eat if anyone else was around. I just kind of zone out with my screen and... yeah.

Yeah, it really does feel like I can't stop. Like, once I start, it feels like I'm just watching myself keep reaching for the next thing, even while I'm thinking, *Jaime, stop, you have a budget report to finish and you're not even enjoying this anymore.* Which reminds me, that budget report was due yesterday, and my boss, Mr. Henderson—oh my gosh, he is so particular about the font sizes on these reports. Um, guilty or ashamed? I'm not really sure, honestly. I haven't really paid attention to the emotional part of it, I guess. Usually, I'm just staring at all the wrappers and empty takeout boxes and realizing I have to gather it all up and take the trash out. 

Which, by the way, is a total nightmare at my apartment building right now. They just forced everyone into this new valet trash service, and they charge us an extra thirty dollars a month for it, but the guy doesn't even pick it up half the time! I live on the third floor, so I guess I get why he wouldn't want to climb all those stairs, but it's literally his job. I tried complaining to the leasing office yesterday, but the girl at the front desk was completely ignoring me. She was just glued to her phone, scrolling through TikTok. I actually saw over her shoulder that she was watching one of those videos where people make those fancy ice cubes with, like, edible flowers and fruit inside? Which did look really pretty, I kind of want to buy one of those big silicone molds now for my iced coffee. But anyway, yeah, I don't know if I feel upset afterward, I haven't really noticed feeling like that.

started.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 19 / financial_manager / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S049\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Yeah, um, well, I guess what made me come in is what happens when I get home after work or class. I've been taking some college courses while working as a financial manager, which is a lot of pressure for a 19-year-old, I know. My boss is always throwing these massive spreadsheets at me right at 4:45 PM, which is just so inconsiderate, right? Like, people want to go home! \n\nBut anyway, I'll finally get home, and I'll sit down with my laptop—usually to watch a show or something, I'm really into that new interior design competition on Netflix right now, the hosts are hilarious—and I'll just end up eating a whole takeout meal. Plus chips, and sweets, and literally whatever snacks are hiding in my desk drawer. It's way, way more than I ever planned to eat. And definitely way more than I’d ever eat if anyone else was around. I just kind of zone out with my screen and... yeah.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating all that food at home, do you feel like you’re not in control of how much you’re eating, or that you can’t stop once you start?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really does feel like I can't stop. They just forced everyone into this new valet trash service, and they charge us an extra thirty dollars a month for it, but the guy doesn't even pick it up half the time! I live on the third floor, so I guess I get why he wouldn't want to climb all those stairs, but it's literally his job. I tried complaining to the leasing office yesterday, but the girl at the front desk was completely ignoring me. She was just glued to her phone, scrolling through TikTok. I actually saw over her shoulder that she was watching one of those videos where people make those fancy ice cubes with, like, edible flowers and fruit inside? Which did look really pretty, I kind of want to buy one of those big silicone molds now for my iced coffee. Yeah, a lot of the time it starts right after I've already had dinner. Like, I'm not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels restless, you know? So I end up going back to the kitchen or ordering more food. 

Actually, speaking of ordering food, I was looking at Uber Eats last night and they completely changed the layout of the app. It's so confusing now! I couldn't even figure out how to filter for vegetarian places. My roommate, Sarah, just decided she's going vegetarian, which is fine, but she threw out all the deli meat in the fridge without even asking me. And I had just bought this really nice honey-glazed turkey from Trader Joe's. I love Trader Joe's, but the parking lot at the one near us is a literal nightmare. The spaces are so tiny, I'm always terrified someone is going to ding my car. I drive a Honda Civic, and it's practically brand new, well, two years old, but I try to keep it looking nice. I even got those custom floor mats for it. But yeah, even when I'm not hungry, I just keep eating because I can't seem to settle my mind down.

I signed up for Planet Fitness like six months ago because they had that ten-dollars-a-month deal, which is honestly such a steal, but the one near my apartment is always so packed. Like, you literally have to wait in line just to use a treadmill. And the other day, I finally got on one, and the guy next to me was sweating so much it was like splashing over onto my machine. It was so gross! I had to wipe the whole thing down with those little paper towels they have, but the dispenser was empty, so I had to go all the way to the front desk to ask for more. And the guy working there was wearing the weirdest neon green shoes. Like, highlighter green. I don't know how people pull off colors like that. I couldn't even figure out how to filter for vegetarian places. My roommate, Sarah, just decided she's going vegetarian, which is fine, but she threw out all the deli meat in the fridge without even asking me. I even got those custom floor mats for it. But yeah, even when I'm not hungry, I just keep eating because I can't seem to settle my mind down.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot more than you plan to ever cause you a lot of distress or interfere with things like school, work, or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it does cause some distress. We usually do these cooking nights on Thursdays, but I've been skipping them because I'm just so worried they're going to notice how much I've been eating on my own, you know? She uses like three different kinds of cheese. But last time she made it, she accidentally used a glass pan that wasn't oven-safe, and it literally shattered everywhere. We were picking glass out of the kitchen rug for an hour. It's one of those really fluffy, shag rugs, too, which are so cute but impossible to clean. I bought a mini vacuum just for my bedroom rug, one of those cordless Dyson ones? It was super expensive, but I had a coupon from Bed Bath & Beyond. I miss when they used to send out those huge 20% off coupons in the mail all the time. My mom used to have a whole drawer dedicated just to keeping those coupons. She’s super organized like that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well, I actually looked back through my journal and my food delivery charges recently to check. And it looks like these episodes have been happening most weeks since the restructuring started at work. Usually more than once a week, too, so it's not just a here and there kind of thing. 

Which, looking at those delivery charges was honestly a little embarrassing because my bank actually flagged my debit card for suspicious activity last week. I use Chase, and their fraud alert texts are so aggressive. They put everything in all caps, so it feels like my phone is yelling at me. It always gives me a mini heart attack. My roommate Sarah actually had her card stolen at a concert last month and someone spent like six hundred dollars at Best Buy. Who even goes to Best Buy anymore? I feel like everyone just buys all their electronics on Amazon now. I share an Amazon Prime account with my mom, mostly for the free two-day shipping, but they actually have some really good shows on Prime Video right now. We started watching this one mystery series, but the lead actor is just so bad at crying on camera, it completely takes me out of the story. Like, they definitely just sprayed water on his face, it didn't look real at all. He was in that one movie from a few years ago... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, my eating. But yeah, it's been happening most weeks, usually a couple times a week.

Um, I'm not really sure if I eat faster than normal. I've never really timed myself or paid that much attention to the speed, to be honest. 

I guess I'm usually just distracted when I eat anyway. Like, I always have to have something playing in the background, usually YouTube. I've been super into these video essays lately where people do like a deep dive into internet drama or old reality TV shows from the 2000s. There's this one channel I found where the girl has the most soothing voice, but her microphone is always just a little too close to her mouth, so you can hear every time she breathes in. It's kind of annoying, but I still watch them. She did this whole hour-long video on the history of MTV's *Cribs*, and it's so funny looking back at what people thought was peak luxury back then. Like, everyone had those giant fish tanks in their living rooms and cars with TVs in the headrests. My uncle actually had a car like that, a really old Honda Odyssey. We used to take it on road trips to Florida when I was a kid, and me and my cousins would fight over who got to pick the DVD. We watched *Shrek* so many times the disc actually stopped working. My cousin Leo was obsessed with it. He actually just got a job at Disney World, which is crazy because he hates the heat. Florida in July is literally a swamp. 

But um... yeah, I haven't really noticed if I'm eating faster. I'm usually just watching videos and not really thinking about it.

Plus chips, and sweets, and literally whatever snacks are hiding in my desk drawer. It's way, way more than I ever planned to eat. And definitely way more than I’d ever eat if anyone else was around. I just kind of zone out with my screen and... yeah.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating all that food at home, do you feel like you’re not in control of how much you’re eating, or that you can’t stop once you start?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really does feel like I can't stop. Like, once I start, it feels like I'm just watching myself keep reaching for the next thing, even while I'm thinking, *Jaime, stop, you have a budget report to finish and you're not even enjoying this anymore.* Which reminds me, that budget report was due yesterday, and my boss, Mr. Henderson—oh my gosh, he is so particular about the font sizes on these reports. He wants everything in Arial size 11, but only for the headers, and the body has to be Calibri size 10. Who even mixes fonts like that? It looks so messy. I tried to tell him that, because I took this graphic design elective last semester and we learned all about typography, but he just brushed me off. He's always doing that. But yeah, I just can't seem to stop myself from eating, even when I know I should.\n\nDoctor: Does this eating behavior tend to leave you feeling upset, guilty, or ashamed afterward?\n\nPatient: Um, guilty or ashamed? I'm not really sure, honestly. I haven't really paid attention to the emotional part of it, I guess. Usually, I'm just staring at all the wrappers and empty takeout boxes and realizing I have to gather it all up and take the trash out. \n\nWhich, by the way, is a total nightmare at my apartment building right now. They just forced everyone into this new valet trash service, and they charge us an extra thirty dollars a month for it, but the guy doesn't even pick it up half the time! I live on the third floor, so I guess I get why he wouldn't want to climb all those stairs, but it's literally his job. I tried complaining to the leasing office yesterday, but the girl at the front desk was completely ignoring me. She was just glued to her phone, scrolling through TikTok. I actually saw over her shoulder that she was watching one of those videos where people make those fancy ice cubes with, like, edible flowers and fruit inside? Which did look really pretty, I kind of want to buy one of those big silicone molds now for my iced coffee. But anyway, yeah, I don't know if I feel upset afterward, I haven't really noticed feeling like that.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of eating more than you intend to, do you ever make yourself vomit, take laxatives, or exercise excessively to try to compensate for the food you ate?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't do any of that. No throwing up or crazy workouts or anything. I mean, I barely have time to go to the gym as it is. I signed up for Planet Fitness like six months ago because they had that ten-dollars-a-month deal, which is honestly such a steal, but the one near my apartment is always so packed. Like, you literally have to wait in line just to use a treadmill. And the other day, I finally got on one, and the guy next to me was sweating so much it was like splashing over onto my machine. It was so gross! I had to wipe the whole thing down with those little paper towels they have, but the dispenser was empty, so I had to go all the way to the front desk to ask for more. And the guy working there was wearing the weirdest neon green shoes. Like, highlighter green. I don't know how people pull off colors like that. I usually just stick to black or white sneakers, you know? They go with everything. Especially for work, since Mr. Henderson is so strict about the dress code. But yeah, no, I don't do any of that stuff after I eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating even when you’re not really hungry, or do you ever eat to help deal with stress or uncomfortable feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot of the time it starts right after I've already had dinner. Like, I'm not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels restless, you know? So I end up going back to the kitchen or ordering more food. \n\nActually, speaking of ordering food, I was looking at Uber Eats last night and they completely changed the layout of the app. It's so confusing now! I couldn't even figure out how to filter for vegetarian places. My roommate, Sarah, just decided she's going vegetarian, which is fine, but she threw out all the deli meat in the fridge without even asking me. And I had just bought this really nice honey-glazed turkey from Trader Joe's. I love Trader Joe's, but the parking lot at the one near us is a literal nightmare. The spaces are so tiny, I'm always terrified someone is going to ding my car. I drive a Honda Civic, and it's practically brand new, well, two years old, but I try to keep it looking nice. I even got those custom floor mats for it. But yeah, even when I'm not hungry, I just keep eating because I can't seem to settle my mind down.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot more than you plan to ever cause you a lot of distress or interfere with things like school, work, or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it does cause some distress. Like, I've actually been avoiding my friends lately. We usually do these cooking nights on Thursdays, but I've been skipping them because I'm just so worried they're going to notice how much I've been eating on my own, you know? So I've just been staying home and isolating myself, outside of going to work and my classes. \n\nWhich, honestly, missing the cooking nights is kind of a bummer because my friend Jessica makes this amazing baked ziti. She uses like three different kinds of cheese. But last time she made it, she accidentally used a glass pan that wasn't oven-safe, and it literally shattered everywhere. We were picking glass out of the kitchen rug for an hour. It's one of those really fluffy, shag rugs, too, which are so cute but impossible to clean. I bought a mini vacuum just for my bedroom rug, one of those cordless Dyson ones? It was super expensive, but I had a coupon from Bed Bath & Beyond. I miss when they used to send out those huge 20% off coupons in the mail all the time. My mom used to have a whole drawer dedicated just to keeping those coupons. She’s super organized like that. But yeah, I’ve just been avoiding everyone so they don't see what's going on with my eating.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating a lot more food than you plan to, like the episodes you’ve described?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I actually looked back through my journal and my food delivery charges recently to check. And it looks like these episodes have been happening most weeks since the restructuring started at work. Usually more than once a week, too, so it's not just a here and there kind of thing. \n\nWhich, looking at those delivery charges was honestly a little embarrassing because my bank actually flagged my debit card for suspicious activity last week. I use Chase, and their fraud alert texts are so aggressive. They put everything in all caps, so it feels like my phone is yelling at me. It always gives me a mini heart attack. My roommate Sarah actually had her card stolen at a concert last month and someone spent like six hundred dollars at Best Buy. Who even goes to Best Buy anymore? I feel like everyone just buys all their electronics on Amazon now. I share an Amazon Prime account with my mom, mostly for the free two-day shipping, but they actually have some really good shows on Prime Video right now. We started watching this one mystery series, but the lead actor is just so bad at crying on camera, it completely takes me out of the story. Like, they definitely just sprayed water on his face, it didn't look real at all. He was in that one movie from a few years ago... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, my eating. But yeah, it's been happening most weeks, usually a couple times a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or bothered by these eating episodes, especially in terms of how they affect your social life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean... kind of like what I was saying before about skipping the cooking nights with my friends? It really does bother me a lot. I just feel so guilty and embarrassed, so I try to just hide in my room and avoid people. \n\nI actually bought these new lavender bath bombs recently to try and relax when I get stressed out about all of it. I got them from Lush, which, if you've ever been in one, the smell literally hits you like a brick wall before you even walk in the door. The one at our mall is right near the main entrance. Though honestly, that whole mall is basically dying anyway. Half the stores are completely boarded up now, and they just replaced the entire food court with this weird indoor glow-in-the-dark mini-golf place. I actually went there with my coworker Dan last month, and he is ridiculously competitive. Like, it's just mini-golf, you know? But he literally brought his own putter. Who even owns a personal mini-golf putter? He works in the same department as me—I'm a financial manager, so we work together a lot—and he’s always setting up these incredibly complicated Excel macros that end up breaking half the time. Then I have to go in and fix his spreadsheets while I'm trying to balance my own college classes. It drives me completely crazy. \n\nBut um... wait, what were we just talking about? Oh, right, feeling bothered by the eating. Yeah, it definitely upsets me a lot, especially since I'm just pushing my friends away.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when you're eating more than you plan to, do you find yourself eating much more quickly than normal?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure if I eat faster than normal. I've never really timed myself or paid that much attention to the speed, to be honest. \n\nI guess I'm usually just distracted when I eat anyway. Like, I always have to have something playing in the background, usually YouTube. I've been super into these video essays lately where people do like a deep dive into internet drama or old reality TV shows from the 2000s. There's this one channel I found where the girl has the most soothing voice, but her microphone is always just a little too close to her mouth, so you can hear every time she breathes in. It's kind of annoying, but I still watch them. She did this whole hour-long video on the history of MTV's *Cribs*, and it's so funny looking back at what people thought was peak luxury back then. Like, everyone had those giant fish tanks in their living rooms and cars with TVs in the headrests. My uncle actually had a car like that, a really old Honda Odyssey. We used to take it on road trips to Florida when I was a kid, and me and my cousins would fight over who got to pick the DVD. We watched *Shrek* so many times the disc actually stopped working. My cousin Leo was obsessed with it. He actually just got a job at Disney World, which is crazy because he hates the heat. Florida in July is literally a swamp. \n\nBut um... yeah, I haven't really noticed if I'm eating faster. I'm usually just watching videos and not really thinking about it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Um, I don't really know if there's a specific trigger, honestly. I haven't really noticed a pattern like that where it's always when I'm stressed or down. 

I guess I don't pay super close attention to my mood right before it happens. I've been trying to track my moods more, actually, because I bought this really nice planner at Target last month. We had one that kept getting into the dumpster behind my apartment complex, and the property manager sent out this very aggressive email about making sure the lids were closed. He writes in that super corporate tone, even though it's just an apartment building. He used the word "behoove" in the email. Like, who says "it would behoove you to secure the refuse receptacles"? Just say close the trash can. It's so pretentious. My English professor talks like that too, actually. He's always assigning these massive reading blocks from Victorian literature, and I swear half the class is just using SparkNotes because nobody has time to read 400 pages of Dickens when they're working full time. 

But, wait... what was the question? Oh, right, about being stressed or upset. Yeah, I haven't really noticed if it's linked to my mood or anything specific like that.

And the guy working there was wearing the weirdest neon green shoes. Like, highlighter green. I don't know how people pull off colors like that. I usually just stick to black or white sneakers, you know? They go with everything. Especially for work, since Mr. Henderson is so strict about the dress code. But yeah, no, I don't do any of that stuff after I eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating even when you’re not really hungry, or do you ever eat to help deal with stress or uncomfortable feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot of the time it starts right after I've already had dinner. Like, I'm not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels restless, you know? So I end up going back to the kitchen or ordering more food. \n\nActually, speaking of ordering food, I was looking at Uber Eats last night and they completely changed the layout of the app. It's so confusing now! I couldn't even figure out how to filter for vegetarian places. My roommate, Sarah, just decided she's going vegetarian, which is fine, but she threw out all the deli meat in the fridge without even asking me. And I had just bought this really nice honey-glazed turkey from Trader Joe's. I love Trader Joe's, but the parking lot at the one near us is a literal nightmare. The spaces are so tiny, I'm always terrified someone is going to ding my car. I drive a Honda Civic, and it's practically brand new, well, two years old, but I try to keep it looking nice. I even got those custom floor mats for it. But yeah, even when I'm not hungry, I just keep eating because I can't seem to settle my mind down.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot more than you plan to ever cause you a lot of distress or interfere with things like school, work, or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it does cause some distress. Like, I've actually been avoiding my friends lately. We usually do these cooking nights on Thursdays, but I've been skipping them because I'm just so worried they're going to notice how much I've been eating on my own, you know? So I've just been staying home and isolating myself, outside of going to work and my classes. \n\nWhich, honestly, missing the cooking nights is kind of a bummer because my friend Jessica makes this amazing baked ziti. She uses like three different kinds of cheese. But last time she made it, she accidentally used a glass pan that wasn't oven-safe, and it literally shattered everywhere. We were picking glass out of the kitchen rug for an hour. It's one of those really fluffy, shag rugs, too, which are so cute but impossible to clean. I bought a mini vacuum just for my bedroom rug, one of those cordless Dyson ones? It was super expensive, but I had a coupon from Bed Bath & Beyond. I miss when they used to send out those huge 20% off coupons in the mail all the time. My mom used to have a whole drawer dedicated just to keeping those coupons. She’s super organized like that. But yeah, I’ve just been avoiding everyone so they don't see what's going on with my eating.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating a lot more food than you plan to, like the episodes you’ve described?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I actually looked back through my journal and my food delivery charges recently to check. I use Chase, and their fraud alert texts are so aggressive. They put everything in all caps, so it feels like my phone is yelling at me. It always gives me a mini heart attack. My roommate Sarah actually had her card stolen at a concert last month and someone spent like six hundred dollars at Best Buy. Who even goes to Best Buy anymore? I feel like everyone just buys all their electronics on Amazon now. I share an Amazon Prime account with my mom, mostly for the free two-day shipping, but they actually have some really good shows on Prime Video right now. We started watching this one mystery series, but the lead actor is just so bad at crying on camera, it completely takes me out of the story. Like, they definitely just sprayed water on his face, it didn't look real at all. He was in that one movie from a few years ago... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, my eating. But yeah, it's been happening most weeks, usually a couple times a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or bothered by these eating episodes, especially in terms of how they affect your social life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean... kind of like what I was saying before about skipping the cooking nights with my friends? Like, everyone had those giant fish tanks in their living rooms and cars with TVs in the headrests. My uncle actually had a car like that, a really old Honda Odyssey. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, actually... a lot of the time it starts right after I've already had dinner. Like, I'm not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels super restless, you know? So I end up just going back into the kitchen or even ordering more food on an app. 

Which, honestly, ordering delivery at my apartment is such a nightmare anyway. The numbering system for the buildings makes absolutely no sense. Building 4 is right next to Building 12, and the drivers always get completely lost. Last week, this guy left my bag at the wrong door, and my neighbor's dog actually got into it. My neighbor has this massive husky that just howls all day long. I feel kind of bad for it because huskies need so much space and exercise, and she just leaves it out on the balcony while she works. I think she's a flight attendant, because I always see her dragging this really nice hard-shell suitcase to her car at like four in the morning. I actually need a new suitcase because the wheel on mine completely snapped off the last time I flew. I tried to fix it with superglue, but it just made this horrible scraping noise the whole way through the airport terminal, and everyone was staring at me. It was so embarrassing. 

But um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating without an appetite. Yeah, I definitely do that. Even if I just finished a full dinner, if my brain is just feeling restless, I'll just keep eating or order more food even though I'm not hungry at all.

I just kind of zone out with my screen and... yeah.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating all that food at home, do you feel like you’re not in control of how much you’re eating, or that you can’t stop once you start?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really does feel like I can't stop. Like, once I start, it feels like I'm just watching myself keep reaching for the next thing, even while I'm thinking, *Jaime, stop, you have a budget report to finish and you're not even enjoying this anymore.* Which reminds me, that budget report was due yesterday, and my boss, Mr. Henderson—oh my gosh, he is so particular about the font sizes on these reports. He wants everything in Arial size 11, but only for the headers, and the body has to be Calibri size 10. Who even mixes fonts like that? It looks so messy. I tried to tell him that, because I took this graphic design elective last semester and we learned all about typography, but he just brushed me off. He's always doing that. But yeah, I just can't seem to stop myself from eating, even when I know I should.\n\nDoctor: Does this eating behavior tend to leave you feeling upset, guilty, or ashamed afterward?\n\nPatient: Um, guilty or ashamed? I'm not really sure, honestly. I haven't really paid attention to the emotional part of it, I guess. Usually, I'm just staring at all the wrappers and empty takeout boxes and realizing I have to gather it all up and take the trash out. \n\nWhich, by the way, is a total nightmare at my apartment building right now. They just forced everyone into this new valet trash service, and they charge us an extra thirty dollars a month for it, but the guy doesn't even pick it up half the time! I live on the third floor, so I guess I get why he wouldn't want to climb all those stairs, but it's literally his job. I tried complaining to the leasing office yesterday, but the girl at the front desk was completely ignoring me. She was just glued to her phone, scrolling through TikTok. I actually saw over her shoulder that she was watching one of those videos where people make those fancy ice cubes with, like, edible flowers and fruit inside? Which did look really pretty, I kind of want to buy one of those big silicone molds now for my iced coffee. But anyway, yeah, I don't know if I feel upset afterward, I haven't really noticed feeling like that.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of eating more than you intend to, do you ever make yourself vomit, take laxatives, or exercise excessively to try to compensate for the food you ate?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't do any of that. No throwing up or crazy workouts or anything. I mean, I barely have time to go to the gym as it is. I signed up for Planet Fitness like six months ago because they had that ten-dollars-a-month deal, which is honestly such a steal, but the one near my apartment is always so packed. Like, you literally have to wait in line just to use a treadmill. And the other day, I finally got on one, and the guy next to me was sweating so much it was like splashing over onto my machine. It was so gross! I had to wipe the whole thing down with those little paper towels they have, but the dispenser was empty, so I had to go all the way to the front desk to ask for more. And the guy working there was wearing the weirdest neon green shoes. Like, highlighter green. I don't know how people pull off colors like that. I usually just stick to black or white sneakers, you know? They go with everything. Especially for work, since Mr. Henderson is so strict about the dress code. But yeah, no, I don't do any of that stuff after I eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating even when you’re not really hungry, or do you ever eat to help deal with stress or uncomfortable feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot of the time it starts right after I've already had dinner. Like, I'm not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels restless, you know? So I end up going back to the kitchen or ordering more food. \n\nActually, speaking of ordering food, I was looking at Uber Eats last night and they completely changed the layout of the app. It's so confusing now! I couldn't even figure out how to filter for vegetarian places. My roommate, Sarah, just decided she's going vegetarian, which is fine, but she threw out all the deli meat in the fridge without even asking me. And I had just bought this really nice honey-glazed turkey from Trader Joe's. I love Trader Joe's, but the parking lot at the one near us is a literal nightmare. The spaces are so tiny, I'm always terrified someone is going to ding my car. I drive a Honda Civic, and it's practically brand new, well, two years old, but I try to keep it looking nice. I even got those custom floor mats for it. But yeah, even when I'm not hungry, I just keep eating because I can't seem to settle my mind down.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot more than you plan to ever cause you a lot of distress or interfere with things like school, work, or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it does cause some distress. Like, I've actually been avoiding my friends lately. We usually do these cooking nights on Thursdays, but I've been skipping them because I'm just so worried they're going to notice how much I've been eating on my own, you know? So I've just been staying home and isolating myself, outside of going to work and my classes. \n\nWhich, honestly, missing the cooking nights is kind of a bummer because my friend Jessica makes this amazing baked ziti. She uses like three different kinds of cheese. But last time she made it, she accidentally used a glass pan that wasn't oven-safe, and it literally shattered everywhere. We were picking glass out of the kitchen rug for an hour. It's one of those really fluffy, shag rugs, too, which are so cute but impossible to clean. I bought a mini vacuum just for my bedroom rug, one of those cordless Dyson ones? It was super expensive, but I had a coupon from Bed Bath & Beyond. I miss when they used to send out those huge 20% off coupons in the mail all the time. My mom used to have a whole drawer dedicated just to keeping those coupons. She’s super organized like that. But yeah, I’ve just been avoiding everyone so they don't see what's going on with my eating.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating a lot more food than you plan to, like the episodes you’ve described?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I actually looked back through my journal and my food delivery charges recently to check. And it looks like these episodes have been happening most weeks since the restructuring started at work. Usually more than once a week, too, so it's not just a here and there kind of thing. \n\nWhich, looking at those delivery charges was honestly a little embarrassing because my bank actually flagged my debit card for suspicious activity last week. I use Chase, and their fraud alert texts are so aggressive. They put everything in all caps, so it feels like my phone is yelling at me. It always gives me a mini heart attack. My roommate Sarah actually had her card stolen at a concert last month and someone spent like six hundred dollars at Best Buy. Who even goes to Best Buy anymore? I feel like everyone just buys all their electronics on Amazon now. I share an Amazon Prime account with my mom, mostly for the free two-day shipping, but they actually have some really good shows on Prime Video right now. We started watching this one mystery series, but the lead actor is just so bad at crying on camera, it completely takes me out of the story. Like, they definitely just sprayed water on his face, it didn't look real at all. He was in that one movie from a few years ago... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, my eating. But yeah, it's been happening most weeks, usually a couple times a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or bothered by these eating episodes, especially in terms of how they affect your social life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean... kind of like what I was saying before about skipping the cooking nights with my friends? It really does bother me a lot. I just feel so guilty and embarrassed, so I try to just hide in my room and avoid people. \n\nI actually bought these new lavender bath bombs recently to try and relax when I get stressed out about all of it. I got them from Lush, which, if you've ever been in one, the smell literally hits you like a brick wall before you even walk in the door. The one at our mall is right near the main entrance. Though honestly, that whole mall is basically dying anyway. Half the stores are completely boarded up now, and they just replaced the entire food court with this weird indoor glow-in-the-dark mini-golf place. I actually went there with my coworker Dan last month, and he is ridiculously competitive. Like, it's just mini-golf, you know? But he literally brought his own putter. Who even owns a personal mini-golf putter? He works in the same department as me—I'm a financial manager, so we work together a lot—and he’s always setting up these incredibly complicated Excel macros that end up breaking half the time. Then I have to go in and fix his spreadsheets while I'm trying to balance my own college classes. It drives me completely crazy. \n\nBut um... wait, what were we just talking about? Oh, right, feeling bothered by the eating. Yeah, it definitely upsets me a lot, especially since I'm just pushing my friends away.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when you're eating more than you plan to, do you find yourself eating much more quickly than normal?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure if I eat faster than normal. I've never really timed myself or paid that much attention to the speed, to be honest. \n\nI guess I'm usually just distracted when I eat anyway. Like, I always have to have something playing in the background, usually YouTube. I've been super into these video essays lately where people do like a deep dive into internet drama or old reality TV shows from the 2000s. There's this one channel I found where the girl has the most soothing voice, but her microphone is always just a little too close to her mouth, so you can hear every time she breathes in. It's kind of annoying, but I still watch them. She did this whole hour-long video on the history of MTV's *Cribs*, and it's so funny looking back at what people thought was peak luxury back then. Like, everyone had those giant fish tanks in their living rooms and cars with TVs in the headrests. My uncle actually had a car like that, a really old Honda Odyssey. We used to take it on road trips to Florida when I was a kid, and me and my cousins would fight over who got to pick the DVD. We watched *Shrek* so many times the disc actually stopped working. My cousin Leo was obsessed with it. He actually just got a job at Disney World, which is crazy because he hates the heat. Florida in July is literally a swamp. \n\nBut um... yeah, I haven't really noticed if I'm eating faster. I'm usually just watching videos and not really thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen when you’re feeling down, stressed, or upset about something specific, like work or school?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know if there's a specific trigger, honestly. I haven't really noticed a pattern like that where it's always when I'm stressed or down. \n\nI guess I don't pay super close attention to my mood right before it happens. I've been trying to track my moods more, actually, because I bought this really nice planner at Target last month. It has all these little habit trackers and mood rings you can color in. Target has been so dangerous lately, though. I went in just to get paper towels and ended up spending like eighty dollars on throw pillows and a new coffee mug. I have so many mugs, but this one had a little raccoon on it, and I just couldn't leave it. Raccoons are honestly kind of cute, even though they're basically trash pandas. We had one that kept getting into the dumpster behind my apartment complex, and the property manager sent out this very aggressive email about making sure the lids were closed. He writes in that super corporate tone, even though it's just an apartment building. He used the word \"behoove\" in the email. Like, who says \"it would behoove you to secure the refuse receptacles\"? Just say close the trash can. It's so pretentious. My English professor talks like that too, actually. He's always assigning these massive reading blocks from Victorian literature, and I swear half the class is just using SparkNotes because nobody has time to read 400 pages of Dickens when they're working full time. \n\nBut, wait... what was the question? Oh, right, about being stressed or upset. Yeah, I haven't really noticed if it's linked to my mood or anything specific like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating even when you don’t have any appetite at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually... a lot of the time it starts right after I've already had dinner. Like, I'm not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels super restless, you know? So I end up just going back into the kitchen or even ordering more food on an app. \n\nWhich, honestly, ordering delivery at my apartment is such a nightmare anyway. The numbering system for the buildings makes absolutely no sense. Building 4 is right next to Building 12, and the drivers always get completely lost. Last week, this guy left my bag at the wrong door, and my neighbor's dog actually got into it. My neighbor has this massive husky that just howls all day long. I feel kind of bad for it because huskies need so much space and exercise, and she just leaves it out on the balcony while she works. I think she's a flight attendant, because I always see her dragging this really nice hard-shell suitcase to her car at like four in the morning. I actually need a new suitcase because the wheel on mine completely snapped off the last time I flew. I tried to fix it with superglue, but it just made this horrible scraping noise the whole way through the airport terminal, and everyone was staring at me. It was so embarrassing. \n\nBut um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating without an appetite. Yeah, I definitely do that. Even if I just finished a full dinner, if my brain is just feeling restless, I'll just keep eating or order more food even though I'm not hungry at all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Yeah, it definitely feels like that. It's like... once I start, I just can't stop. It feels like I'm watching myself keep reaching for the next thing, even while I'm thinking, *Jaime, stop, you have a budget report to finish and you're not even enjoying this anymore.*

He just kept telling me to clear my cache, which I obviously already did. I'm not an idiot. I actually built my own computer when I was in high school, so I know how basic troubleshooting works. I used to be really into gaming back then. I played so much World of Warcraft that I actually failed my sophomore year biology class because I was staying up until 4 AM doing raids. My parents were so mad. They actually took my power cord and locked it in their safe. Which was kind of an overreaction, but I guess I get it. My mom is super intense about academics. I only have like three semesters left, but working full time makes it so hard to even think about taking classes again.

Like, people want to go home! \n\nBut anyway, I'll finally get home, and I'll sit down with my laptop—usually to watch a show or something, I'm really into that new interior design competition on Netflix right now, the hosts are hilarious—and I'll just end up eating a whole takeout meal. Plus chips, and sweets, and literally whatever snacks are hiding in my desk drawer. It's way, way more than I ever planned to eat. And definitely way more than I’d ever eat if anyone else was around. I just kind of zone out with my screen and... yeah.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating all that food at home, do you feel like you’re not in control of how much you’re eating, or that you can’t stop once you start?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really does feel like I can't stop. Like, once I start, it feels like I'm just watching myself keep reaching for the next thing, even while I'm thinking, *Jaime, stop, you have a budget report to finish and you're not even enjoying this anymore.* Which reminds me, that budget report was due yesterday, and my boss, Mr. Henderson—oh my gosh, he is so particular about the font sizes on these reports. He wants everything in Arial size 11, but only for the headers, and the body has to be Calibri size 10. Who even mixes fonts like that? It looks so messy. I tried to tell him that, because I took this graphic design elective last semester and we learned all about typography, but he just brushed me off. He's always doing that. But yeah, I just can't seem to stop myself from eating, even when I know I should.\n\nDoctor: Does this eating behavior tend to leave you feeling upset, guilty, or ashamed afterward?\n\nPatient: Um, guilty or ashamed? I'm not really sure, honestly. I haven't really paid attention to the emotional part of it, I guess. Usually, I'm just staring at all the wrappers and empty takeout boxes and realizing I have to gather it all up and take the trash out. \n\nWhich, by the way, is a total nightmare at my apartment building right now. They just forced everyone into this new valet trash service, and they charge us an extra thirty dollars a month for it, but the guy doesn't even pick it up half the time! I live on the third floor, so I guess I get why he wouldn't want to climb all those stairs, but it's literally his job. I tried complaining to the leasing office yesterday, but the girl at the front desk was completely ignoring me. She was just glued to her phone, scrolling through TikTok. I actually saw over her shoulder that she was watching one of those videos where people make those fancy ice cubes with, like, edible flowers and fruit inside? Which did look really pretty, I kind of want to buy one of those big silicone molds now for my iced coffee. But anyway, yeah, I don't know if I feel upset afterward, I haven't really noticed feeling like that.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes of eating more than you intend to, do you ever make yourself vomit, take laxatives, or exercise excessively to try to compensate for the food you ate?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't do any of that. No throwing up or crazy workouts or anything. I mean, I barely have time to go to the gym as it is. I signed up for Planet Fitness like six months ago because they had that ten-dollars-a-month deal, which is honestly such a steal, but the one near my apartment is always so packed. Like, you literally have to wait in line just to use a treadmill. And the other day, I finally got on one, and the guy next to me was sweating so much it was like splashing over onto my machine. It was so gross! I had to wipe the whole thing down with those little paper towels they have, but the dispenser was empty, so I had to go all the way to the front desk to ask for more. And the guy working there was wearing the weirdest neon green shoes. Like, highlighter green. I don't know how people pull off colors like that. I usually just stick to black or white sneakers, you know? They go with everything. Especially for work, since Mr. Henderson is so strict about the dress code. But yeah, no, I don't do any of that stuff after I eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating even when you’re not really hungry, or do you ever eat to help deal with stress or uncomfortable feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot of the time it starts right after I've already had dinner. Like, I'm not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels restless, you know? So I end up going back to the kitchen or ordering more food. \n\nActually, speaking of ordering food, I was looking at Uber Eats last night and they completely changed the layout of the app. It's so confusing now! I couldn't even figure out how to filter for vegetarian places. My roommate, Sarah, just decided she's going vegetarian, which is fine, but she threw out all the deli meat in the fridge without even asking me. And I had just bought this really nice honey-glazed turkey from Trader Joe's. I love Trader Joe's, but the parking lot at the one near us is a literal nightmare. The spaces are so tiny, I'm always terrified someone is going to ding my car. I drive a Honda Civic, and it's practically brand new, well, two years old, but I try to keep it looking nice. I even got those custom floor mats for it. But yeah, even when I'm not hungry, I just keep eating because I can't seem to settle my mind down.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot more than you plan to ever cause you a lot of distress or interfere with things like school, work, or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it does cause some distress. Like, I've actually been avoiding my friends lately. We usually do these cooking nights on Thursdays, but I've been skipping them because I'm just so worried they're going to notice how much I've been eating on my own, you know? So I've just been staying home and isolating myself, outside of going to work and my classes. \n\nWhich, honestly, missing the cooking nights is kind of a bummer because my friend Jessica makes this amazing baked ziti. She uses like three different kinds of cheese. But last time she made it, she accidentally used a glass pan that wasn't oven-safe, and it literally shattered everywhere. We were picking glass out of the kitchen rug for an hour. It's one of those really fluffy, shag rugs, too, which are so cute but impossible to clean. I bought a mini vacuum just for my bedroom rug, one of those cordless Dyson ones? It was super expensive, but I had a coupon from Bed Bath & Beyond. I miss when they used to send out those huge 20% off coupons in the mail all the time. My mom used to have a whole drawer dedicated just to keeping those coupons. She’s super organized like that. But yeah, I’ve just been avoiding everyone so they don't see what's going on with my eating.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating a lot more food than you plan to, like the episodes you’ve described?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I actually looked back through my journal and my food delivery charges recently to check. And it looks like these episodes have been happening most weeks since the restructuring started at work. Usually more than once a week, too, so it's not just a here and there kind of thing. \n\nWhich, looking at those delivery charges was honestly a little embarrassing because my bank actually flagged my debit card for suspicious activity last week. I use Chase, and their fraud alert texts are so aggressive. They put everything in all caps, so it feels like my phone is yelling at me. It always gives me a mini heart attack. My roommate Sarah actually had her card stolen at a concert last month and someone spent like six hundred dollars at Best Buy. Who even goes to Best Buy anymore? I feel like everyone just buys all their electronics on Amazon now. I share an Amazon Prime account with my mom, mostly for the free two-day shipping, but they actually have some really good shows on Prime Video right now. We started watching this one mystery series, but the lead actor is just so bad at crying on camera, it completely takes me out of the story. Like, they definitely just sprayed water on his face, it didn't look real at all. He was in that one movie from a few years ago... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, my eating. But yeah, it's been happening most weeks, usually a couple times a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or bothered by these eating episodes, especially in terms of how they affect your social life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean... kind of like what I was saying before about skipping the cooking nights with my friends? It really does bother me a lot. I just feel so guilty and embarrassed, so I try to just hide in my room and avoid people. \n\nI actually bought these new lavender bath bombs recently to try and relax when I get stressed out about all of it. I got them from Lush, which, if you've ever been in one, the smell literally hits you like a brick wall before you even walk in the door. The one at our mall is right near the main entrance. Though honestly, that whole mall is basically dying anyway. Half the stores are completely boarded up now, and they just replaced the entire food court with this weird indoor glow-in-the-dark mini-golf place. I actually went there with my coworker Dan last month, and he is ridiculously competitive. Like, it's just mini-golf, you know? But he literally brought his own putter. Who even owns a personal mini-golf putter? He works in the same department as me—I'm a financial manager, so we work together a lot—and he’s always setting up these incredibly complicated Excel macros that end up breaking half the time. Then I have to go in and fix his spreadsheets while I'm trying to balance my own college classes. It drives me completely crazy. \n\nBut um... wait, what were we just talking about? Oh, right, feeling bothered by the eating. Yeah, it definitely upsets me a lot, especially since I'm just pushing my friends away.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when you're eating more than you plan to, do you find yourself eating much more quickly than normal?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure if I eat faster than normal. I've never really timed myself or paid that much attention to the speed, to be honest. \n\nI guess I'm usually just distracted when I eat anyway. Like, I always have to have something playing in the background, usually YouTube. I've been super into these video essays lately where people do like a deep dive into internet drama or old reality TV shows from the 2000s. There's this one channel I found where the girl has the most soothing voice, but her microphone is always just a little too close to her mouth, so you can hear every time she breathes in. It's kind of annoying, but I still watch them. She did this whole hour-long video on the history of MTV's *Cribs*, and it's so funny looking back at what people thought was peak luxury back then. Like, everyone had those giant fish tanks in their living rooms and cars with TVs in the headrests. My uncle actually had a car like that, a really old Honda Odyssey. We used to take it on road trips to Florida when I was a kid, and me and my cousins would fight over who got to pick the DVD. We watched *Shrek* so many times the disc actually stopped working. My cousin Leo was obsessed with it. He actually just got a job at Disney World, which is crazy because he hates the heat. Florida in July is literally a swamp. \n\nBut um... yeah, I haven't really noticed if I'm eating faster. I'm usually just watching videos and not really thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen when you’re feeling down, stressed, or upset about something specific, like work or school?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know if there's a specific trigger, honestly. I haven't really noticed a pattern like that where it's always when I'm stressed or down. \n\nI guess I don't pay super close attention to my mood right before it happens. I've been trying to track my moods more, actually, because I bought this really nice planner at Target last month. It has all these little habit trackers and mood rings you can color in. Target has been so dangerous lately, though. I went in just to get paper towels and ended up spending like eighty dollars on throw pillows and a new coffee mug. I have so many mugs, but this one had a little raccoon on it, and I just couldn't leave it. Raccoons are honestly kind of cute, even though they're basically trash pandas. We had one that kept getting into the dumpster behind my apartment complex, and the property manager sent out this very aggressive email about making sure the lids were closed. He writes in that super corporate tone, even though it's just an apartment building. He used the word \"behoove\" in the email. Like, who says \"it would behoove you to secure the refuse receptacles\"? Just say close the trash can. It's so pretentious. My English professor talks like that too, actually. He's always assigning these massive reading blocks from Victorian literature, and I swear half the class is just using SparkNotes because nobody has time to read 400 pages of Dickens when they're working full time. \n\nBut, wait... what was the question? Oh, right, about being stressed or upset. Yeah, I haven't really noticed if it's linked to my mood or anything specific like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating even when you don’t have any appetite at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually... a lot of the time it starts right after I've already had dinner. I feel kind of bad for it because huskies need so much space and exercise, and she just leaves it out on the balcony while she works. I think she's a flight attendant, because I always see her dragging this really nice hard-shell suitcase to her car at like four in the morning. I actually need a new suitcase because the wheel on mine completely snapped off the last time I flew. I tried to fix it with superglue, but it just made this horrible scraping noise the whole way through the airport terminal, and everyone was staring at me. My boss just changed the whole formatting for how we submit them, and the new software they bought is so glitchy. It keeps crashing every time I try to export the files to a PDF. I had to call IT like three times last week, and the guy on the phone was so unhelpful. He just kept telling me to clear my cache, which I obviously already did. I'm not an idiot. I actually built my own computer when I was in high school, so I know how basic troubleshooting works. I used to be really into gaming back then. I played so much World of Warcraft that I actually failed my sophomore year biology class because I was staying up until 4 AM doing raids. My parents were so mad. They actually took my power cord and locked it in their safe. Which was kind of an overreaction, but I guess I get it. My mom is super intense about academics. She's a high school principal, so she's always on me about going back and finishing my degree. I just can't stop myself once I get going, even when I know I should.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen when you’re feeling down or depressed, or do they seem to happen more when you're stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: I know we kind of touched on this a minute ago, but yeah, I honestly still couldn't tell you if it's because I'm depressed or if it's anxiety. I really haven't noticed a specific emotion like that causing it. \n\nI actually tried downloading one of those mindfulness apps recently to see if it would help me figure out my stress levels, you know, the one with the really soothing voice? I had to go to the mall this weekend to get it replaced at one of those little kiosks. The guy working there was so incredibly pushy, trying to get me to buy this ridiculous rhinestone phone case. I used to love stuff like that when I was in middle school, but it's just so impractical now. Plus, those liquid glitter cases are actually super dangerous. Fashion moves so fast, it's exhausting just trying to buy jeans that don't look completely outdated. \n\nBut um... sorry, I'm doing it again. You were asking if it's anxiety or feeling down. I know we just talked about this, but like I said, I really don't know if it's depression or anxiety or what. I just haven't noticed a specific emotion tied to it. 

I did try to start tracking my moods in this little journal I bought at Target. It has this really pretty floral cover, but the paper quality is actually terrible. It was so gross! I had to wipe the whole thing down with those little paper towels they have, but the dispenser was empty, so I had to go all the way to the front desk to ask for more. And the guy working there was wearing the weirdest neon green shoes. Like, highlighter green. I don't know how people pull off colors like that. I usually just stick to black or white sneakers, you know? They go with everything. Especially for work, since Mr. Henderson is so strict about the dress code. But yeah, no, I don't do any of that stuff after I eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating even when you’re not really hungry, or do you ever eat to help deal with stress or uncomfortable feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot of the time it starts right after I've already had dinner. Like, I'm not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels restless, you know? So I end up going back to the kitchen or ordering more food. \n\nActually, speaking of ordering food, I was looking at Uber Eats last night and they completely changed the layout of the app. It's so confusing now! I couldn't even figure out how to filter for vegetarian places. My roommate, Sarah, just decided she's going vegetarian, which is fine, but she threw out all the deli meat in the fridge without even asking me. And I had just bought this really nice honey-glazed turkey from Trader Joe's. I love Trader Joe's, but the parking lot at the one near us is a literal nightmare. The spaces are so tiny, I'm always terrified someone is going to ding my car. I drive a Honda Civic, and it's practically brand new, well, two years old, but I try to keep it looking nice. I even got those custom floor mats for it. But yeah, even when I'm not hungry, I just keep eating because I can't seem to settle my mind down.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot more than you plan to ever cause you a lot of distress or interfere with things like school, work, or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it does cause some distress. Like, I've actually been avoiding my friends lately. We usually do these cooking nights on Thursdays, but I've been skipping them because I'm just so worried they're going to notice how much I've been eating on my own, you know? So I've just been staying home and isolating myself, outside of going to work and my classes. \n\nWhich, honestly, missing the cooking nights is kind of a bummer because my friend Jessica makes this amazing baked ziti. She uses like three different kinds of cheese. But last time she made it, she accidentally used a glass pan that wasn't oven-safe, and it literally shattered everywhere. We were picking glass out of the kitchen rug for an hour. It's one of those really fluffy, shag rugs, too, which are so cute but impossible to clean. I bought a mini vacuum just for my bedroom rug, one of those cordless Dyson ones? It was super expensive, but I had a coupon from Bed Bath & Beyond. I miss when they used to send out those huge 20% off coupons in the mail all the time. My mom used to have a whole drawer dedicated just to keeping those coupons. She’s super organized like that. But yeah, I’ve just been avoiding everyone so they don't see what's going on with my eating.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating a lot more food than you plan to, like the episodes you’ve described?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I actually looked back through my journal and my food delivery charges recently to check. And it looks like these episodes have been happening most weeks since the restructuring started at work. Usually more than once a week, too, so it's not just a here and there kind of thing. \n\nWhich, looking at those delivery charges was honestly a little embarrassing because my bank actually flagged my debit card for suspicious activity last week. I use Chase, and their fraud alert texts are so aggressive. They put everything in all caps, so it feels like my phone is yelling at me. It always gives me a mini heart attack. My roommate Sarah actually had her card stolen at a concert last month and someone spent like six hundred dollars at Best Buy. Who even goes to Best Buy anymore? I feel like everyone just buys all their electronics on Amazon now. I share an Amazon Prime account with my mom, mostly for the free two-day shipping, but they actually have some really good shows on Prime Video right now. We started watching this one mystery series, but the lead actor is just so bad at crying on camera, it completely takes me out of the story. Like, they definitely just sprayed water on his face, it didn't look real at all. He was in that one movie from a few years ago... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, my eating. But yeah, it's been happening most weeks, usually a couple times a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or bothered by these eating episodes, especially in terms of how they affect your social life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean... kind of like what I was saying before about skipping the cooking nights with my friends? It really does bother me a lot. I just feel so guilty and embarrassed, so I try to just hide in my room and avoid people. \n\nI actually bought these new lavender bath bombs recently to try and relax when I get stressed out about all of it. There's this one channel I found where the girl has the most soothing voice, but her microphone is always just a little too close to her mouth, so you can hear every time she breathes in. It's kind of annoying, but I still watch them. She did this whole hour-long video on the history of MTV's *Cribs*, and it's so funny looking back at what people thought was peak luxury back then. Like, everyone had those giant fish tanks in their living rooms and cars with TVs in the headrests. My uncle actually had a car like that, a really old Honda Odyssey. We used to take it on road trips to Florida when I was a kid, and me and my cousins would fight over who got to pick the DVD. We watched *Shrek* so many times the disc actually stopped working. My cousin Leo was obsessed with it. He actually just got a job at Disney World, which is crazy because he hates the heat. Florida in July is literally a swamp. \n\nBut um... yeah, I haven't really noticed if I'm eating faster. Um, no, I haven't really felt down like that. I still look forward to things. Like, I actually just watched the new episode of that interior design show last night. The one where they redo the whole kitchen? It was so good. But they used this horrible olive green tile for the backsplash, and I just couldn't believe it. Who chooses olive green for a kitchen? Like, you literally have to wait in line just to use a treadmill. And the other day, I finally got on one, and the guy next to me was sweating so much it was like splashing over onto my machine. It was so gross! I had to wipe the whole thing down with those little paper towels they have, but the dispenser was empty, so I had to go all the way to the front desk to ask for more. And the guy working there was wearing the weirdest neon green shoes. Like, highlighter green. I don't know how people pull off colors like that. I usually just stick to black or white sneakers, you know? They go with everything. Especially for work, since Mr. Henderson is so strict about the dress code. But yeah, no, I don't do any of that stuff after I eat.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating even when you’re not really hungry, or do you ever eat to help deal with stress or uncomfortable feelings?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot of the time it starts right after I've already had dinner. Like, I'm not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels restless, you know? So I end up going back to the kitchen or ordering more food. \n\nActually, speaking of ordering food, I was looking at Uber Eats last night and they completely changed the layout of the app. It's so confusing now! I couldn't even figure out how to filter for vegetarian places. My roommate, Sarah, just decided she's going vegetarian, which is fine, but she threw out all the deli meat in the fridge without even asking me. And I had just bought this really nice honey-glazed turkey from Trader Joe's. I love Trader Joe's, but the parking lot at the one near us is a literal nightmare. The spaces are so tiny, I'm always terrified someone is going to ding my car. I drive a Honda Civic, and it's practically brand new, well, two years old, but I try to keep it looking nice. I even got those custom floor mats for it. But yeah, even when I'm not hungry, I just keep eating because I can't seem to settle my mind down.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating a lot more than you plan to ever cause you a lot of distress or interfere with things like school, work, or spending time with friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess it does cause some distress. Like, I've actually been avoiding my friends lately. We usually do these cooking nights on Thursdays, but I've been skipping them because I'm just so worried they're going to notice how much I've been eating on my own, you know? So I've just been staying home and isolating myself, outside of going to work and my classes. \n\nWhich, honestly, missing the cooking nights is kind of a bummer because my friend Jessica makes this amazing baked ziti. She uses like three different kinds of cheese. But last time she made it, she accidentally used a glass pan that wasn't oven-safe, and it literally shattered everywhere. We were picking glass out of the kitchen rug for an hour. It's one of those really fluffy, shag rugs, too, which are so cute but impossible to clean. I bought a mini vacuum just for my bedroom rug, one of those cordless Dyson ones? It was super expensive, but I had a coupon from Bed Bath & Beyond. I miss when they used to send out those huge 20% off coupons in the mail all the time. My mom used to have a whole drawer dedicated just to keeping those coupons. She’s super organized like that. But yeah, I’ve just been avoiding everyone so they don't see what's going on with my eating.\n\nDoctor: How often do you find yourself eating a lot more food than you plan to, like the episodes you’ve described?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I actually looked back through my journal and my food delivery charges recently to check. And it looks like these episodes have been happening most weeks since the restructuring started at work. Usually more than once a week, too, so it's not just a here and there kind of thing. \n\nWhich, looking at those delivery charges was honestly a little embarrassing because my bank actually flagged my debit card for suspicious activity last week. I use Chase, and their fraud alert texts are so aggressive. They put everything in all caps, so it feels like my phone is yelling at me. It always gives me a mini heart attack. My roommate Sarah actually had her card stolen at a concert last month and someone spent like six hundred dollars at Best Buy. Who even goes to Best Buy anymore? I feel like everyone just buys all their electronics on Amazon now. I share an Amazon Prime account with my mom, mostly for the free two-day shipping, but they actually have some really good shows on Prime Video right now. We started watching this one mystery series, but the lead actor is just so bad at crying on camera, it completely takes me out of the story. Like, they definitely just sprayed water on his face, it didn't look real at all. He was in that one movie from a few years ago... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, my eating. But yeah, it's been happening most weeks, usually a couple times a week.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel distressed or bothered by these eating episodes, especially in terms of how they affect your social life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I mean... kind of like what I was saying before about skipping the cooking nights with my friends? It really does bother me a lot. I just feel so guilty and embarrassed, so I try to just hide in my room and avoid people. \n\nI actually bought these new lavender bath bombs recently to try and relax when I get stressed out about all of it. I got them from Lush, which, if you've ever been in one, the smell literally hits you like a brick wall before you even walk in the door. The one at our mall is right near the main entrance. Though honestly, that whole mall is basically dying anyway. Half the stores are completely boarded up now, and they just replaced the entire food court with this weird indoor glow-in-the-dark mini-golf place. I actually went there with my coworker Dan last month, and he is ridiculously competitive. Like, it's just mini-golf, you know? But he literally brought his own putter. Who even owns a personal mini-golf putter? I've never really timed myself or paid that much attention to the speed, to be honest. \n\nI guess I'm usually just distracted when I eat anyway. Like, I always have to have something playing in the background, usually YouTube. I've been super into these video essays lately where people do like a deep dive into internet drama or old reality TV shows from the 2000s. There's this one channel I found where the girl has the most soothing voice, but her microphone is always just a little too close to her mouth, so you can hear every time she breathes in. It's kind of annoying, but I still watch them. She did this whole hour-long video on the history of MTV's *Cribs*, and it's so funny looking back at what people thought was peak luxury back then. Like, everyone had those giant fish tanks in their living rooms and cars with TVs in the headrests. My uncle actually had a car like that, a really old Honda Odyssey. We used to take it on road trips to Florida when I was a kid, and me and my cousins would fight over who got to pick the DVD. We watched *Shrek* so many times the disc actually stopped working. My cousin Leo was obsessed with it. He actually just got a job at Disney World, which is crazy because he hates the heat. Florida in July is literally a swamp. \n\nBut um... yeah, I haven't really noticed if I'm eating faster. I'm usually just watching videos and not really thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen when you’re feeling down, stressed, or upset about something specific, like work or school?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know if there's a specific trigger, honestly. I haven't really noticed a pattern like that where it's always when I'm stressed or down. \n\nI guess I don't pay super close attention to my mood right before it happens. Target has been so dangerous lately, though. I went in just to get paper towels and ended up spending like eighty dollars on throw pillows and a new coffee mug. I have so many mugs, but this one had a little raccoon on it, and I just couldn't leave it. Raccoons are honestly kind of cute, even though they're basically trash pandas. I played so much World of Warcraft that I actually failed my sophomore year biology class because I was staying up until 4 AM doing raids. My parents were so mad. I just can't stop myself once I get going, even when I know I should.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen when you’re feeling down or depressed, or do they seem to happen more when you're stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: I know we kind of touched on this a minute ago, but yeah, I honestly still couldn't tell you if it's because I'm depressed or if it's anxiety. Yeah, a lot of the time it actually starts right after I’ve already had dinner. I’ll be completely full, like not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels really restless, you know? So I end up going back to the kitchen or I'll just order more food. 

Which is honestly getting so frustrating because the delivery fees on those apps are completely out of control now. And the driver didn't even follow the delivery instructions. My neighbor down the hall has this really cute welcome mat with a picture of a dachshund on it, and the delivery guys always leave her packages right on the dog's face. I love dachshunds, they have those little short legs. My family had one growing up named Barnaby, but he had horrible back problems. The vet said it's super common for that breed because their spines are just too long. We had to build him these little wooden ramps so he could get up on the couch without jumping.
