There's this one channel I found where the girl has the most soothing voice, but her microphone is always just a little too close to her mouth, so you can hear every time she breathes in. It's kind of annoying, but I still watch them. She did this whole hour-long video on the history of MTV's *Cribs*, and it's so funny looking back at what people thought was peak luxury back then. Like, everyone had those giant fish tanks in their living rooms and cars with TVs in the headrests. My uncle actually had a car like that, a really old Honda Odyssey. We used to take it on road trips to Florida when I was a kid, and me and my cousins would fight over who got to pick the DVD. We watched *Shrek* so many times the disc actually stopped working. My cousin Leo was obsessed with it. He actually just got a job at Disney World, which is crazy because he hates the heat. Florida in July is literally a swamp. \n\nBut um... yeah, I haven't really noticed if I'm eating faster. I'm usually just watching videos and not really thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen when you’re feeling down, stressed, or upset about something specific, like work or school?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't really know if there's a specific trigger, honestly. I haven't really noticed a pattern like that where it's always when I'm stressed or down. \n\nI guess I don't pay super close attention to my mood right before it happens. I've been trying to track my moods more, actually, because I bought this really nice planner at Target last month. It has all these little habit trackers and mood rings you can color in. Target has been so dangerous lately, though. I went in just to get paper towels and ended up spending like eighty dollars on throw pillows and a new coffee mug. I have so many mugs, but this one had a little raccoon on it, and I just couldn't leave it. Raccoons are honestly kind of cute, even though they're basically trash pandas. We had one that kept getting into the dumpster behind my apartment complex, and the property manager sent out this very aggressive email about making sure the lids were closed. He writes in that super corporate tone, even though it's just an apartment building. He used the word \"behoove\" in the email. Like, who says \"it would behoove you to secure the refuse receptacles\"? Just say close the trash can. It's so pretentious. My English professor talks like that too, actually. He's always assigning these massive reading blocks from Victorian literature, and I swear half the class is just using SparkNotes because nobody has time to read 400 pages of Dickens when they're working full time. \n\nBut, wait... what was the question? Oh, right, about being stressed or upset. Yeah, I haven't really noticed if it's linked to my mood or anything specific like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating even when you don’t have any appetite at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually... a lot of the time it starts right after I've already had dinner. Like, I'm not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels super restless, you know? So I end up just going back into the kitchen or even ordering more food on an app. \n\nWhich, honestly, ordering delivery at my apartment is such a nightmare anyway. The numbering system for the buildings makes absolutely no sense. Building 4 is right next to Building 12, and the drivers always get completely lost. Last week, this guy left my bag at the wrong door, and my neighbor's dog actually got into it. My neighbor has this massive husky that just howls all day long. I feel kind of bad for it because huskies need so much space and exercise, and she just leaves it out on the balcony while she works. I think she's a flight attendant, because I always see her dragging this really nice hard-shell suitcase to her car at like four in the morning. I actually need a new suitcase because the wheel on mine completely snapped off the last time I flew. I tried to fix it with superglue, but it just made this horrible scraping noise the whole way through the airport terminal, and everyone was staring at me. It was so embarrassing. \n\nBut um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, eating without an appetite. Yeah, I definitely do that. Even if I just finished a full dinner, if my brain is just feeling restless, I'll just keep eating or order more food even though I'm not hungry at all.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when you eat more than you plan to, do you feel like you’ve lost control over your eating, like you can’t stop once you start?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely feels like that. It's like... once I start, I just can't stop. It feels like I'm watching myself keep reaching for the next thing, even while I'm thinking, *Jaime, stop, you have a budget report to finish and you're not even enjoying this anymore.* \n\nBut the budget reports have been so stressful lately. My boss just changed the whole formatting for how we submit them, and the new software they bought is so glitchy. It keeps crashing every time I try to export the files to a PDF. I had to call IT like three times last week, and the guy on the phone was so unhelpful. He just kept telling me to clear my cache, which I obviously already did. I'm not an idiot. I actually built my own computer when I was in high school, so I know how basic troubleshooting works. I used to be really into gaming back then. I played so much World of Warcraft that I actually failed my sophomore year biology class because I was staying up until 4 AM doing raids. My parents were so mad. They actually took my power cord and locked it in their safe. Which was kind of an overreaction, but I guess I get it. My mom is super intense about academics. She's a high school principal, so she's always on me about going back and finishing my degree. I only have like three semesters left, but working full time makes it so hard to even think about taking classes again. \n\nBut um... yeah, I definitely feel like I lose control. I just can't stop myself once I get going, even when I know I should.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen when you’re feeling down or depressed, or do they seem to happen more when you're stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: I know we kind of touched on this a minute ago, but yeah, I honestly still couldn't tell you if it's because I'm depressed or if it's anxiety. I really haven't noticed a specific emotion like that causing it. \n\nI actually tried downloading one of those mindfulness apps recently to see if it would help me figure out my stress levels, you know, the one with the really soothing voice? But I was lying in bed trying to listen to the sleep story and I completely dropped my phone on my face. It hurt so bad, and it actually cracked my screen protector right down the middle. I had to go to the mall this weekend to get it replaced at one of those little kiosks. The guy working there was so incredibly pushy, trying to get me to buy this ridiculous rhinestone phone case. I used to love stuff like that when I was in middle school, but it's just so impractical now. Plus, those liquid glitter cases are actually super dangerous. My friend Sarah had one in high school that cracked and leaked this weird chemical oil all over her favorite jeans. We spent like three hours in her bathroom trying to scrub it out with baking soda, but it completely ruined them. She was devastated because they were from Abercrombie, and we had to save up for months to afford anything from there back then. I don't even know if Abercrombie is still popular now, honestly. Fashion moves so fast, it's exhausting just trying to buy jeans that don't look completely outdated. \n\nBut um... sorry, I'm doing it again. You were asking if it's anxiety or feeling down. Yeah, I just really don't know. I haven't been able to pinpoint a specific feeling like that before it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen when you're feeling down or depressed, or do they seem to happen more when you're stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: I know we just talked about this, but like I said, I really don't know if it's depression or anxiety or what. I just haven't noticed a specific emotion tied to it. \n\nI did try to start tracking my moods in this little journal I bought at Target. It has this really pretty floral cover, but the paper quality is actually terrible. Every time I try to use my favorite gel pens, the ink bleeds right through to the other side. It's so frustrating because I have this huge collection of gel pens from when I was obsessed with bullet journaling a couple of years ago. I spent so much money on all these different colors and stencils, but I gave up on it after like two weeks because it just took way too much time to draw all those little boxes and calendars. I don't know how people on Instagram make it look so effortless. They must not have full-time jobs, because by the time I get home from work, the last thing I want to do is sit down with a ruler and measure out a grid for my habit tracker. I tried to give all my supplies to my little cousin, but she said she only uses her iPad for art now. Kids are so weird with technology these days. They don't even know the joy of going to the office supply store and picking out a fresh notebook. \n\nBut um... yeah, sorry, I'm rambling again. You were asking about feeling down or stressed. Like I said, I just really haven't been able to figure out if a certain mood is causing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed feeling down or losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like your interior design show or cooking nights with friends, for most of the day, more days than not, in the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't really felt down like that. I still look forward to things. Like, I actually just watched the new episode of that interior design show last night. The one where they redo the whole kitchen? It was so good. But they used this horrible olive green tile for the backsplash, and I just couldn't believe it. Who chooses olive green for a kitchen? It totally clashed with the countertops. I would have gone with a classic white subway tile, maybe with a dark grout if they wanted it to pop. Or even a marble slab if they had the budget. But I guess they were trying to be \"trendy\" or whatever. It's like that time I tried to paint my bedroom this dark navy blue because I saw it on Pinterest and thought it would look so moody and chic. But it ended up just looking like a cave. I had to buy three cans of primer just to cover it up and go back to a normal beige. Painting is honestly such a hassle. You have to tape everything off, and no matter how careful you are, you always end up with specks of paint on the trim or the floor. I actually got a drop of that navy paint on my favorite pair of fuzzy socks, and it never came out. I still wear them, though, because they're so warm. They have these little grippy things on the bottom so you don't slip on hardwood floors. \n\nBut um, yeah, anyway... no, I haven't been feeling depressed or losing interest in things. I still enjoy my shows and hanging out with my friends.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these eating episodes are causing you significant distress or making it hard for you to function in your daily life, like at work, school, or with your relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it definitely bothers me. It's causing problems, mostly with my relationships, I guess. Like, I’ve been avoiding my friends and family a lot lately. I used to love our cooking nights, but now I just make up excuses not to go because I’m terrified they’ll somehow notice how much I’ve been eating when I'm alone. So I've just been really isolated outside of my classes and my job. \n\nWhich is actually super awkward right now because my friend Jessica just got this new apartment downtown, and she's been trying to get everyone over for a housewarming thing. She's been texting the group chat non-stop about it. She got this amazing deal on rent because it's above a bakery, which sounds great until you realize you're going to smell croissants at 4 AM every single day. I told her she's going to get mice, because my aunt lived above a restaurant once and she had to get an exterminator out like three times in one year. The exterminator guy actually told her that mice can squeeze through a hole the size of a dime, which is just terrifying. I can't even look at a dime the same way anymore. I tried to use a handful of change to buy a coffee the other day because I left my debit card in my other purse, and I swear I stared at the dimes for a solid minute thinking about mice. The barista probably thought I was crazy. He was this new guy, too, and he completely messed up my order. I asked for oat milk and he definitely used almond milk. I didn't say anything because I hate sending things back, but almond milk always leaves this weird aftertaste, you know? \n\nBut um, anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the distress thing. Yeah, I've just been staying away from everyone because I don't want them to figure out what's going on with my eating. It's just easier to be alone right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself eating even when you’re not physically hungry at all?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a lot of the time it actually starts right after I’ve already had dinner. I’ll be completely full, like not physically hungry at all, but my mind just feels really restless, you know? So I end up going back to the kitchen or I'll just order more food. \n\nWhich is honestly getting so frustrating because the delivery fees on those apps are completely out of control now. I ordered a single burrito the other night, and with the service fee, the small order fee, and the tip, it was almost thirty dollars. For one burrito! And the driver didn't even follow the delivery instructions. I specifically asked them to leave it on the little metal chair by my door, but he put it right on the doormat so I had to squeeze my arm out the crack of the door to grab it without knocking it over. It’s like they don’t even look at the notes. My neighbor down the hall has this really cute welcome mat with a picture of a dachshund on it, and the delivery guys always leave her packages right on the dog's face. I love dachshunds, they have those little short legs. My family had one growing up named Barnaby, but he had horrible back problems. The vet said it's super common for that breed because their spines are just too long. We had to build him these little wooden ramps so he could get up on the couch without jumping. My dad painted them to match the living room baseboards, which was nice, but Barnaby was so stubborn he'd just try to jump anyway. \n\nBut um... sorry, I lost track of what I was saying again. Oh, right, eating without being hungry. Yeah, it's usually just after dinner when I can't get my brain to settle down, so I just keep getting more food even though my stomach is totally full.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or lost interest in things you usually enjoy—like your interior design show or time with friends—for most of the day, more days than not, in the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um, wait, didn't you just ask me that? Though I am still so annoyed about that olive green tile. I was actually thinking about it this morning when I was making coffee. My coffee maker is this really sleek silver one, which is so much better than the bulky black one I used to have. The black one always showed every single water spot, and no matter how much I wiped it down with those microfiber cloths, it never looked clean. I bought a whole pack of those cloths at Costco once, thinking I'd use them for everything, but they have this weird texture that catches on your skin if your hands are even slightly dry. It's the worst feeling, like nails on a chalkboard but on your hands. My mom used to hate the sound of nails on a chalkboard, she'd literally cringe and cover her ears. She was a teacher for a few years before she went back to school for accounting, so I guess she heard it a lot. Accounting sounds so boring, honestly. Staring at spreadsheets all day would drive me crazy. \n\nBut um... yeah, like I said before, I'm not feeling down or losing interest in things. I'm just mostly staying away from friends right now because of the eating stuff, not because I don't want to see them."
